Maternal vaccination is also reducing intensive care admissions. </h3><p>In the most severe cases, babies with RSV may develop serious breathing difficulties and need to be admitted to intensive care, where they can receive more advanced breathing support. </p><p>The <a href="https://www.eurosurveillance.org/content/10.2807/1560-7917.ES.2026.31.36.2600752#r1">English study</a> found that intensive care admissions for bronchiolitis among babies younger than three months were 41% lower than expected following the introduction of maternal vaccination – although this figure included bronchiolitis caused by any virus, not just RSV. </p><h3>4. Real-world studies are providing reassurance about vaccine safety</h3><p>Clinical trials provided important evidence about the safety of maternal RSV vaccination, but continuing to monitor larger numbers of women and babies in real-world settings can help to identify rare side-effects that might not have been detected during trials. </p><p>A systematic review and meta-analysis – published in <a href="https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S2666776226002590#sec1"><em>The Lancet Regional Health – Europe</em></a> – combined data from three real-world studies involving more than 16,000 pregnancies, and found no evidence that vaccination between 32 and 35 weeks of pregnancy increased the risk of premature birth. </p><p>The researchers concluded that the safety profile of the vaccine was favourable. </p> </div> </div> </div> </div> </div> <p> <a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" href="https://www.gavi.org/vaccineswork/rsv-vaccines-are-leading-far-fewer-babies-hospitals-4-things-you-need-know">Original article</a> </p><p>This article was originally published on <a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" href="https://www.gavi.org/vaccineswork">VaccinesWork</a> </p><script>(function(w,d,s,l,i){w[l]=w[l]||[];w[l].push({'gtm.start': new Date().getTime(),event:'gtm.js'});var f=d.getElementsByTagName(s)[0], j=d.createElement(s),dl=l!='dataLayer'?'&l='+l:'';j.async=true;j.src= 'https://www.googletagmanager.com/gtm.js?id='+i+dl;f.parentNode.insertBefore(j,f); })(window,document,'script','dataLayer','GTM-M7H54VD');</script><noscript><iframe src="https://www.googletagmanager.com/ns.html?id=GTM-M7H54VD" height="0" width="0" style="display:none;visibility:hidden"></iframe></noscript></article> Add as preferred Google source A newborn baby in hospital. Credit: amranAydinov on Magnific At a glance A recent study found that maternal RSV vaccination reduced babies’ risk of being hospitalised with RSV-related lower respiratory tract infections by 73.6%. Countries that have introduced maternal RSV vaccination programmes are also seeing reductions in hospitalisations at a population level. In England, admissions for RSV-related bronchiolitis among babies younger than three months were 62.6% lower between September 2024 and March 2026 than would have been expected without vaccination. This study also found that intensive care admissions for bronchiolitis among babies younger than three months were 41% lower than expected following the introduction of maternal vaccination. Three years after the first maternal respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) vaccines began being rolled out, mounting real-world evidence shows the difference they’re making to babies’ health. Studies from some of the first countries to introduce maternal RSV vaccination suggest that babies whose mothers were vaccinated have a 70–80% lower risk of being hospitalised with RSV than those whose mothers weren’t. Meanwhile, national studies are recording substantial falls in hospital admissions since vaccination programmes began. Here are four things we’ve learned so far. 1. Maternal vaccination cuts babies’ risk of being hospitalised with RSV by nearly three quarters. RSV is a common virus that affects the nose, throat and lungs. Although most infections are mild, it is a leading cause of lung infections and hospitalisation in young children worldwide. Babies are particularly vulnerable because their airways are small and their immune systems are still developing. Clinical trials established that maternal vaccination could protect babies against serious RSV illness during their first months of life, by allowing protective antibodies to pass from mother to baby across the placenta. Now, real-world studies are revealing how well this protection holds up across larger and more diverse populations. A recent systematic review and meta-analysis published in The Lancet Regional Health – Europe , combined evidence from 21 real-world studies of maternal RSV vaccination published between July 2024 and September 2025. Most of the studies came from the US, with others from the UK, Argentina and France. When the researchers pooled data from four of these studies that specifically assessed vaccine effectiveness against hospitalisation in babies under six months old, they found that RSV vaccines reduced babies’ risk of being hospitalised with RSV-related lower respiratory tract infections by 73.6%. 2. Fewer babies are being hospitalised with RSV-related illness at the regional and national level. Countries that have introduced maternal RSV vaccination programmes are also beginning to see benefits at a population level, with substantial reductions in hospital admissions for RSV-related illness among young infants. In England, where maternal RSV vaccination was introduced in September 2024, researchers recently compared hospital admissions among babies, following the programme’s introduction with the numbers expected based on historical trends. The study, published in Eurosurveillance, found that admissions for RSV-related bronchiolitis (a lung infection that can cause breathing difficulties) among babies younger than three months were 62.6% lower than expected between September 2024 and March 2026, even though maternal vaccination only averaged around 55% during this period. RSV vaccines reduced babies’ risk of being hospitalised with RSV-related lower respiratory tract infections by 73.6%. In Argentina, researchers examined admissions for RSV-related lower respiratory tract infections among babies younger than six months at hospitals in Buenos Aires . They estimated that admissions were 33.6% lower than expected following the introduction of maternal vaccination in 2024. Although the reductions reported in England and Argentina differed, the studies examined different age groups and used different methods, making direct comparisons difficult. Other factors, including differences in RSV circulation between seasons may also have influenced hospital admission rates. Even so, the findings suggest that maternal vaccination programmes are helping to reduce the burden of serious RSV illness on hospitals, as well as protecting individual babies against the virus. Have you read? Major evidence review confirms influenza, COVID-19 and RSV vaccines are safe and effective Largest study yet shows RSV vaccine in pregnancy cuts babies’ hospital risk by 80% 3. Maternal vaccination is also reducing intensive care admissions. In the most severe cases, babies with RSV may develop serious breathing difficulties and need to be admitted to intensive care, where they can receive more advanced breathing support. The English study found that intensive care admissions for bronchiolitis among babies younger than three months were 41% lower than expected following the introduction of maternal vaccination – although this figure included bronchiolitis caused by any virus, not just RSV. 4. Real-world studies are providing reassurance about vaccine safety Clinical trials provided important evidence about the safety of maternal RSV vaccination, but continuing to monitor larger numbers of women and babies in real-world settings can help to identify rare side-effects that might not have been detected during trials. A systematic review and meta-analysis – published in The Lancet Regional Health – Europe – combined data from three real-world studies involving more than 16,000 pregnancies, and found no evidence that vaccination between 32 and 35 weeks of pregnancy increased the risk of premature birth. The researchers concluded that the safety profile of the vaccine was favourable. View five news elements
Time
2 November 2026 (publication date), September 2024 to March 2026 (event period).
Location and population
England, Buenos Aires, and other regions including the US, UK, Argentina, and France. Target entity: respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and its related strains (RSV-A, RSV-B).
Event
Maternal RSV vaccination is associated with reduced hospital admissions for RSV-related lower respiratory tract infections in babies. This is confirmed by real-world studies and a systematic review and meta-analysis. The event is reported by Linda Geddes, Gavi.org, and Eurosurveillance.
Scale, impact and risk
In England, hospital admissions for RSV-related bronchiolitis among babies under three months were 62.6% lower than expected between September 2024 and March 2026. Intensive care admissions for bronchiolitis were 41% lower. In Buenos Aires, admissions were 33.6% lower. The risk reduction is explicitly stated as 73.6% for hospitalisation with RSV-related lower respiratory tract infections.
Response, status and uncertainty
Real-world studies are ongoing, and findings are based on pooled data from multiple studies. The studies used different age groups and methods, making direct comparisons difficult. The evidence is supported by a systematic review and meta-analysis, and no significant safety concerns were reported.

马来西亚住院儿童的呼吸道合胞病毒季节性在大流行中断后反弹 - Bioengineer.org

RSV seasonality in hospitalized Malaysian children rebounded after pandemic disruption - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报A study published on October 29, 2026, by Bioengineer.org reveals that respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) seasonality in hospitalized Malaysian children rebounded after pandemic disruption. Researchers from Malaysia’s Institute for Medical Research, Hospital Tuanku Ja’afar Seremban, and other Ministry of Health institutions analyzed eight years of laboratory surveillance data from 2017 to 2024, covering 417 continuous weeks. They found that RSV positivity rates were 14.29 percent, with historical peaks between July and September before the pandemic. The pandemic years (2020-2022) disrupted this pattern, but it re-established in 2024. The study used ARIMAX modeling to identify factors influencing RSV dynamics, including public holidays and influenza activity. While major holidays were associated with a temporary decrease in cases followed by a surge, influenza showed a one-week lagged association. The authors caution that these are statistical correlations, not proven biological mechanisms, and the findings may not apply to community cases or other regions. The research highlights the importance of monitoring influenza as an early indicator for RSV waves and underscores the need for localized immunization planning in tropical settings like Malaysia.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究分析了 2017 年第 1 周至 2024 年第 52 周的流行病学数据,并于 2026 年 10 月 29 日报告了结果。大流行中断发生在 2020 年至 2022 年期间,RSV 季节性在 2024 年重新建立。
地点与对象
该研究针对马来西亚森美兰州 12 岁以下住院儿童,从 2017 年到 2024 年连续 417 周进行。
事件
根据对实验室 监测 数据的八年分析观察到,马来西亚森美兰州住院儿童的 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 季节性在 COVID-19 大流行期间被破坏,并于 2024 年重新建立。这项研究是由马来西亚医学研究所、芙蓉端姑嘉化医院和卫生部其他机构的研究人员进行的。
规模、影响与风险
Across the study period, 6,545 out of 45,817 tested samples were positive for RSV, resulting in a positivity rate of 14.29 percent. Before the pandemic, RSV cases historically peaked between July and September. The study found that major public holidays had a dual effect: cases decreased during the holiday period (p = 0.012) and then surged afterward (p = 0.014). Influenza activity was also a significant predictor of RSV cases with a one-week lag (p = 0.012).
应对、状态与不确定性
The study used time-series decomposition,autocorrelation functions,and an ARIMAX model to analyze RSV seasonality and its drivers. The authors emphasized that these are modeled associations from observational data and do not establish causation directly.由于该研究的重点是马来西亚某个州的住院儿童,因此研究结果可能无法推广到社区病例、门诊疾病或其他地区。

RSV seasonality in hospitalized Malaysian children rebounded after pandemic disruption - Bioengineer.org

Researchers from Malaysia’s Institute for Medical Research, Hospital Tuanku Ja’afar Seremban, and other Ministry of Health institutions analyzed eight years of laboratory surveillance data from 2017 to 2024, covering 417 continuous weeks. They found that RSV positivity rates were 14.29 percent, with historical peaks between July and September before the pandemic. The study used ARIMAX modeling to identify factors influencing RSV dynamics, including public holidays and influenza activity. HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, November 4, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health RSV seasonality in hospitalized Malaysian children rebounded after pandemic disruption by October 29, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 RSV seasonality in hospitalized Malaysian children rebounded after pandemic disruption Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) showed distinct seasonal peaks among hospitalized children in Negeri Sembilan, Malaysia, that were disrupted during the COVID-19 pandemic years and re-established in 2024, according to an eight-year analysis of laboratory surveillance data. The study, conducted by researchers from Malaysia’s Institute for Medical Research, Hospital Tuanku Ja’afar Seremban and other Ministry of Health institutions, examined weekly RSV detections among children under 12 years old admitted with acute lower respiratory tract infections between epidemiological week 1 of 2017 and week 52 of 2024, spanning 417 continuous weeks. Across the study period, 6,545 of 45,817 tested samples were positive for RSV, a positivity rate of 14.29 percent. Before the pandemic, cases historically peaked between July and September. To disentangle the drivers of RSV transmission, the team used time-series decomposition and autocorrelation functions to characterize seasonality, then applied an autoregressive integrated moving average model with exogenous variables, ARIMAX (2,0,4)(1,0,0)52, to test the influence of co-circulating viruses, meteorological conditions and public holidays. The modeling indicated that RSV dynamics were driven primarily by internal autoregressive patterns, meaning recent levels of RSV activity themselves were the strongest predictor of subsequent cases, rather than by meteorological factors. Discover more respiratory virus respiratory syncytial virus Find Lung Care Respiratory syncytial virus Check Symptoms Two exogenous factors did show statistically significant associations. Major public holidays had a dual effect: reported cases decreased immediately during the holiday period (p = 0.012) and then surged afterward (p = 0.014). The authors interpret this pattern as consistent with changes in social mixing, with reduced contact during holidays followed by increased transmission when gatherings and travel resume. Because these are modeled associations from observational surveillance data, they do not establish causation directly. Influenza activity was also a significant predictor of RSV cases with a one-week lag (p = 0.012). This finding is consistent with the hypothesis of viral interference, in which infection with one respiratory virus may temporarily modulate susceptibility to or transmission of another, although the observational design cannot confirm a causal mechanism. The study’s setting matters for interpretation. Unlike temperate regions with pronounced winter RSV seasons, tropical settings such as Malaysia experience RSV year-round with seasonal fluctuations, making locally derived timing data especially relevant for immunization planning. The analysis has limitations inherent to its design. It covers a single Malaysian state and relies on hospitalized pediatric patients with lower respiratory tract infections, so the findings may not generalize to community cases, outpatient illness or other regions. The ARIMAX framework captures statistical associations and lagged relationships, not proven biological mechanisms. Discover more Read Med Journals Track Outbreaks Find Family Lawyer Hire Babysitters infections For public health planners, the practical signals are the restored July-to-September peak, the predictable post-holiday surge in cases, and the potential value of monitoring influenza activity as an early indicator for RSV waves. Time
The study analyzed data from epidemiological week 1 of 2017 to week 52 of 2024, with the findings reported on October 29, 2026. The pandemic disruption occurred during the years 2020-2022, and the RSV seasonality re-established in 2024.
Location and population
The study focused on hospitalized children under 12 years old in Negeri Sembilan, Malaysia, across 417 continuous weeks from 2017 to 2024.
Event
Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) seasonality among hospitalized children in Negeri Sembilan, Malaysia, was disrupted during the COVID-19 pandemic years and re-established in 2024, as observed through an eight-year analysis of laboratory surveillance data. The study was conducted by researchers from Malaysia’s Institute for Medical Research, Hospital Tuanku Ja’afar Seremban, and other Ministry of Health institutions.
Scale, impact and risk
Across the study period, 6,545 out of 45,817 tested samples were positive for RSV, resulting in a positivity rate of 14.29 percent. Before the pandemic, RSV cases historically peaked between July and September. The study found that major public holidays had a dual effect: cases decreased during the holiday period (p = 0.012) and then surged afterward (p = 0.014). Influenza activity was also a significant predictor of RSV cases with a one-week lag (p = 0.012).
Response, status and uncertainty
The study used time-series decomposition, autocorrelation functions, and an ARIMAX model to analyze RSV seasonality and its drivers. The authors emphasized that these are modeled associations from observational data and do not establish causation directly. The findings may not generalize to community cases, outpatient illness, or other regions due to the study's focus on hospitalized children in a single Malaysian state.

您的孩子现在是否患有感冒或危及生命的“模仿”病毒?发现差异的 5 种方法

Does your child have a cold or life-threatening ‘copycat’ virus spreading right now? 5 ways to spot the difference
新闻简报The article published on November 3, 2026, by Bing News RSS discusses the rise of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) in the UK, highlighting its potential severity for children, especially babies under one year old, and vulnerable groups. RSV activity increased in the week leading up to October 25, 2026, with a 1.5 per cent rise in positive lab tests compared to the previous week. Hospital admissions for RSV-related illness increased to 0.52 per 100,000, up from 0.31 per 100,000 the prior week. The UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) notes that while most RSV cases are mild, they can lead to serious conditions like bronchiolitis or pneumonia. The article emphasizes the importance of recognizing severe symptoms such as difficulty breathing, high fever, or poor feeding, which require immediate medical attention. It also mentions that RSV is the leading cause of infant mortality globally and that an estimated 36 children under 18 died of RSV-related causes between October 2025 and April 2026, compared to 78 in the same period last year. Prevention measures include good hand hygiene, avoiding close contact with sick individuals, and vaccination for pregnant women and older adults with certain health conditions. The article serves as a guide for parents and caregivers to differentiate between a common cold and a more serious RSV infection.
查看新闻五要素
时间
The article was published on 2026-11-03. RSV activity increased in the week leading up to October 25, 2026, with a 1.5 per cent increase in positive lab tests compared to the previous week. Hospital admissions for RSV-related illness increased to 0.52 per 100,000, compared with 0.31 per 100,000 in the previous week.
地点与对象
英国,特别关注 18 岁以下的儿童以及早产儿和具有某些健康状况的人群等弱势群体。文章还提到了老年人面临的风险,特别是 75 岁至 79 岁的老年人以及 2024 年 9 月 1 日后年满 80 岁的老年人。
事件
英国 RSV 感染正在增加,英国卫生安全局 (UKHSA) 报告称 RSV 活动处于基线水平。文章讨论了呼吸道合胞病毒的症状及其与普通感冒的区别,强调严重病例需要就医。
规模、影响与风险
RSV is the leading cause of infant mortality globally. In the UK, an estimated 36 children and teens under 18 died of RSV-related causes between October 2025 and April 2026. This is compared to 78 deaths in the same period last year and 52 in the 2023 to 2024 season. Hospital admissions for RSV-related illness increased to 0.52 per 100,000, and emergency department attendances for acute bronchiolitis were stable and below seasonally expected levels.
应对、状态与不确定性
UKHSA 正在监测 RSV 活动,该文章提供了识别症状和寻求医疗帮助的指导。英国于 2024 年 9 月推出了针对孕妇的 疫苗接种 计划,以保护新生儿。文章还概述了预防措施,例如良好的手部卫生、避免与病人密切接触以及保持水分。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Does your child have a cold or life-threatening ‘copycat’ virus spreading right now? 5 ways to spot the difference

News Brief
The article published on November 3, 2026, by Bing News RSS discusses the rise of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) in the UK, highlighting its potential severity for children, especially babies under one year old, and vulnerable groups. RSV activity increased in the week leading up to October 25, 2026, with a 1.5 per cent rise in positive lab tests compared to the previous week. Hospital admissions for RSV-related illness increased to 0.52 per 100,000, up from 0.31 per 100,000 the prior week. The UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) notes that while most RSV cases are mild, they can lead to serious conditions like bronchiolitis or pneumonia. The article emphasizes the importance of recognizing severe symptoms such as difficulty breathing, high fever, or poor feeding, which require immediate medical attention. It also mentions that RSV is the leading cause of infant mortality globally and that an estimated 36 children under 18 died of RSV-related causes between October 2025 and April 2026, compared to 78 in the same period last year. Prevention measures include good hand hygiene, avoiding close contact with sick individuals, and vaccination for pregnant women and older adults with certain health conditions. The article serves as a guide for parents and caregivers to differentiate between a common cold and a more serious RSV infection.
Original source text (English)
IT’S that time of year where everyone seems to have caught some sort of bug – so it’s no surprise if your tot has got a cough or snotty nose. But are your child’s niggles caused by a simple cold, or have they picked up a ‘copycat’ virus currently ticking up in the UK, which can be life-threatening to kids? From difficulty breathing to lack of appetite – read on for key signs indicating your child has a more serious infection Source: Getty RSV – which stands for respiratory syncytial virus – is a very common bug that often causes coughs and colds . It infects around 90 per cent of children within the first few years of their life and typically causes mild symptoms, such as a runny nose , sneezing and coughing . Sign up to The Irish Sun’s daily newsletter Thank you! RSV infections usually get better by themselves, but they can sometimes become serious, especially for babies. The UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) recently said that RSV infections were slowly ramping up . “RSV activity increased and is circulating at baseline levels,” it wrote in its report for the week leading up to October 25. The number of positive lab tests increased by 1.5 per cent, compared by 0.9 the previous week. Meanwhile, the number of positive swabs at GPs saw a small increase too. Hospital admissions for RSV-related illness also increased to a rate of 0.52 per 100,000, compared with 0.31 per 100,000 in the previous week. Emergency department attendances for acute bronchiolitis – which is a type of chest infection affecting babies that can cause breathing problems – were “stable and below seasonally expected levels”, the UKHSA added. Dr Raj Arora, a GP at The Fairmont Windsor Park and founder of The Facebible , says: “RSV is serious primarily for young children, especially babies under a year old, as it can cause bronchiolitis.” According to the NHS, bronchiolitis may need to be treated in hospital. RSV can also lead to a serious lung infection such as pneumonia. Dr Arora adds: “Premature infants and those with certain health conditions are at higher risk of severe illness.” So, how can you tell the difference between a harmless cold and a serious RSV infection? There are some telltale signs to watch out for, according to Dr Raj. She tells parents: “RSV causes cold- like symptoms such asa runny nose, coughing, and fever, but it can develop into more severe respiratory illness in babies and vulnerable children.” Milder symptoms will include: A runny or blocked nose A cough Sneezing Tiredness A high temperature – signs include back or chest feeling hotter than usual, sweatiness and shivering Babies with RSV may also be irritable and feed less than usual. But symptoms getting worse could mean the infection is progressing to something more serious, such as bronchiolitis or pneumonia. Dr Arora says: “It’s important to watch for difficulty breathing, high fever, poor feeding, or extreme lethargy in infants, these require immediate medical attention. “While most colds resolve within one-to-two weeks with supportive care, persistent symptoms or worsening conditions warrant [getting] medical advice.” The NHS advises parents to look out for the following symptoms: A cough that gets worse Shortness of breath Faster breathing or long gaps between breaths Difficulty feeding in babies or loss of appetite in older children Noisy breathing, also known as wheezing Get immediate medical help if you’re worried your little one is seriously ill. According to the UKHSA, RSV is the leading cause of infant mortality globally. In the UK, an estimated 36 children and teens under the age of 18 died of RSV-related causes between October 2025 and April 2026. For comparison, 78 deaths were reported in children during the same period last year, and 52 in the 2023 to 2024 season. Call 999 if: Your child is having difficulty breathing – you may notice grunting noises, long pauses in their breathing or their tummy sucking under their ribs You have severe difficulty breathing – you’re gasping, choking or not able to get words out You or your child is floppy and will not wake up or stay awake You or your child’s lips or skin are turning very pale, blue or grey – on brown or black skin, this may be easier to see on the palms of the hands Your child is under five years old and has a temperature below 36C As a parent, you may know if your child seems seriously unwell and should trust your judgement. Source: NHS Can I prevent RSV infections in my kids? There are ways to help prevent your little one getting seriously ill with RSV. Dr Arora advises practicing “good hand hygiene”, so making sure your tot’s hands are clean and stopping them from touching their eyes, nose or mouth before washing. The GP also recommended “avoiding close contact with sick individuals” and getting vaccinated against the bug. An RSV vaccination programme for pregnant women was introduced in England in September 2024, to give vulnerable new-born babies protection against severe RSV disease. Mums-to-be can get their jab from 28 weeks onwards, until the end of their pregnancy. The virus can also be dangerous to older adults, so people aged 75 to 79 – and those who turned 80 years old after September 1, 2024 – can also get the vaccine for free on the NHS. Adults aged 65 to 74 with a chronic respiratory condition or suppressed immune system were made eligible for a free jab in September. This includes thousands of people living with chronic asthma , bronchitis or cystic fibrosis or who have weakened immunity due to diabetes , blood conditions or medical treatments like chemotherapy. “RSV seasons can vary, but awareness and readiness to seek care if severe symptoms develop are crucial,” Dr Arora adds. “The rise in RSV cases calls for awareness especially among vulnerable groups to protect your family.” If you or your tot are feeling poorly, the GP advised: “Get plenty of rest so your body can fight the virus. “Drink lots of fluids such as water, and broth to stay hydrated and loosen mucus. “Use a humidifier or inhale steam to ease congestion. Gargle with salt water (your child should not do this). “Use over the counter cold medicines as directed by doctor or pharmacist. “Maintain good nutrition, even if appetite is reduced.”

The article also outlines prevention measures such as good hand hygiene, avoiding close contact with sick individuals, and staying hydrated.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

RSV 免疫接种与婴儿住院率下降有关

RSV Immunization Linked to Drop in Infant Hospitalizations
新闻简报2026 年 10 月 29 日在法国里昂举行的 CEPAS 2026 大会上发表的一项研究发现,使用 尼塞韦单抗 或孕产妇 疫苗接种 对抗 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 的国家计划与婴儿住院治疗的显着减少有关。该研究分析了 2022 年 6 月至 2026 年 5 月连续四个 RSV 季节中与 RSV 相关的下呼吸道感染 (LRTI)。在西班牙,该计划使 0-12 个月婴儿的住院人数平均减少了 66.9%,其中 0-6 个月婴儿的住院人数平均减少了 75.7%。英国平均减少 32.5%,其中 0-6 个月婴儿减少 39.8%。荷兰报告下降了 46.4%,其中 0-6 个月婴儿下降了 56.1%。西班牙和英国的 ICU 入院人数分别下降了 74.5% 和 49.7%。早产儿因 RSV 相关住院的风险增加 10 倍。该研究强调了欧洲国家免疫策略的巨大差异,一些国家提供普遍保护,另一些则根据胎龄进行限制。研究结果表明,在保护早产儿免受呼吸道合胞病毒和流感感染方面仍有改进的空间。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026年10月29日在CEPAS 2026大会上提交。分析的RSV季节跨度为2022年6月至2026年5月,流行病学年定义为6月1日至5月31日。英国于2024年9月推出了RSV预防计划,荷兰于2025年秋季推出了该计划。
地点与对象
该研究在西班牙、荷兰和英国进行。该研究重点关注因下呼吸道感染 (LRTI) 或 RSV 相关 LRTI 住院的 24 个月或更小的婴儿,特别是早产儿。
事件
两项欧洲研究表明,使用 尼塞韦单抗 或孕产妇 疫苗接种 对抗 RSV 的国家计划与婴儿住院治疗的大幅减少有关。研究结果已在 CEPAS 2026 大会上公布。这些研究分析了连续四个 RSV 季节与 RSV 相关的 LRTI 住院情况。
规模、影响与风险
在西班牙,RSV 预防计划使 0-12 个月婴儿因 RSV 相关 LRTI 住院治疗平均减少了 66.9%,其中 0-6 个月婴儿减少了 75.7%。在英国,平均下降了 32.5%,其中 0-6 个月婴儿下降了 39.8%。在荷兰,下降了 46.4%,其中 0-6 个月婴儿下降了 56.1%。西班牙和英国的 ICU 入院人数分别下降了 74.5% 和 49.7%。早产儿因 RSV 相关住院的风险增加 10 倍。
应对、状态与不确定性
这些研究是回顾性、观察性的,并使用常规医院管理数据,在某些情况下还使用实验室数据。研究人员绘制了 11 个欧洲国家的免疫策略。策略存在很大差异,一些国家提供普遍保护,而另一些国家则根据胎龄进行限制。研究结果表明早产儿的保护策略还有改进的空间。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

RSV Immunization Linked to Drop in Infant Hospitalizations

News Brief
A study presented at the CEPAS 2026 Congress in Lyon, France, on October 29, 2026, found that national programs using nirsevimab or maternal vaccination against respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) are associated with significant reductions in infant hospitalizations. The research analyzed RSV-related lower respiratory tract infections (LRTI) across four consecutive RSV seasons from June 2022 to May 2026. In Spain, the program led to a 66.9% average reduction in hospitalizations for infants aged 0-12 months, with a 75.7% drop in those aged 0-6 months. The UK saw a 32.5% average reduction, with a 39.8% decrease in infants aged 0-6 months. The Netherlands reported a 46.4% reduction, with a 56.1% drop in infants aged 0-6 months. ICU admissions in Spain and the UK fell by 74.5% and 49.7%, respectively. Preterm infants had a 10-fold increased risk of RSV-related hospitalizations. The study highlighted substantial variation in immunization strategies across European countries, with some offering universal protection and others restricting based on gestational age. The findings suggest that there is still room for improvement in protecting preterm children against RSV and influenza.
Original source text (English)
Your Clinical Hub News & Perspective Tools & Reference CME/CE More Video Events Specialties Topics RegisterLog In Ask AI Search Medscape News EuropeConference NewsCEPAS 2026 RSV Immunization Linked to Drop in Infant Hospitalizations Liam Davenport October 30, 2026 0 LYON, France — National programs using nirsevimab or maternal vaccination against respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) are associated with large reductions in infant hospitalizations, but there is wide variation in how they are implemented between countries, particularly for children born preterm, show two European studies. The research was presented at the Congress of the European Paediatric Academic Societies (CEPAS) 2026 on October 29. Jeroen Geurtsen, MSc, of Global Medical Vaccines at Sanofi B.V. in Amsterdam, Netherlands, pointed out that although long-acting monoclonal antibodies such as nirsevimab have been available since 2022, “European countries have implemented these in different ways.” He explained that in Spain, nirsevimab immunization was introduced for all infants in autumn 2023, followed by the same universal coverage in Netherlands from autumn 2025, although maternal vaccination had been available out of pocket from December 2023. In September 2024, the UK introduced year-round maternal RSV vaccination, adding nirsevimab for high-risk infants and those born before 32 weeks’ gestation from September 2025. To examine the real-world public health impact of these programs, the researchers used routine hospital administrative data and, for some institutions, laboratory data to perform a retrospective, observational study of the three countries. They included infants aged 24 months or younger who were hospitalized with lower respiratory tract infection (LRTI) or RSV-related LRTI, and looked at four consecutive RSV seasons between June 2022 and May 2026, with the epidemiologic year defined as June 1 to May 31. Specifically, they collected information on the infants’ 24-month pre-index medical history, index hospitalization details, and 30-day postdischarge healthcare utilization. Geurtsen and colleagues showed that just under half of hospital admissions were in infants aged 0-6 months and a further one quarter occurred in those aged 7-12 months. The mean age at admission ranged from 7 to 9 months, and around three fifths of infants were male. The results showed that in Spain, the RSV prevention program was associated with a 66.9% average reduction in RSV-related LRTI hospitalizations among infants aged 0-12 months across three post-implementation seasons, rising to 75.7% in those aged 0-6 months vs 36.8% in those aged 7-12 months. In the UK, the RSV prevention program was linked to a 32.5% average reduction in RSV-related LRTI hospitalizations among infants aged 0-12 months across two postimplementation seasons. A decrease of 39.8% was seen in infants aged 0-6 months vs 5.1% in those aged 7-12 months. In Netherlands, introduction of the RSV program was associated with a 46.4% decrease in RSV-related LRTI hospitalizations among infants aged 0-12 months over a single postimplementation season. This included a 56.1% drop in infants aged 0-6 months vs 26.5% in those aged 7-12 months. ICU data from Spain and the UK indicated that ICU admissions in infants aged 0-12 months fell by 74.5% and 49.7%, respectively, although median ICU length of stay was largely unchanged. In a separate presentation, Sara Marie Nilsen, PhD, of the Department of Clinical and Molecular Medicine at Norwegian University of Science and Technology in Trondheim, Norway, presented a mapping of immunization strategies against RSV and influenza in children born preterm. She told the audience that “preterm born children are at increased risk of respiratory tract infections requiring hospitalization,” with a 10-fold increased risk in extremely preterm children (those born before 28 weeks’ gestation). This increased risk “extends beyond infancy and into early childhood.” To examine how European countries are addressing this risk, she and her colleagues mapped immunization strategies across 11 countries participating in the European Union-funded IMPROVE PRETERM project: Denmark, Estonia, Finland, France, Germany, Norway, Poland, Portugal, Sweden, Switzerland, and the UK. A structured questionnaire was completed and validated by national clinicians and vaccination experts, focusing on strategies for RSV and influenza in effect for the 2025-2026 season. For RSV immunization, the researchers found a mix of approaches between the countries. France, Finland, Germany, Portugal, Sweden, and Switzerland offered universal protection for all infants, while the remaining five countries had some form of restriction based on weeks of gestation. In addition, Denmark, France, Poland, Switzerland, and the UK had a free national maternal vaccination program. In the other six countries, this was not part of a free national program. For a child’s second RSV season, only Switzerland had a specific strategy for children born before 32 weeks’ gestation; the remainder had no gestational age-based eligibility for immunization. For influenza immunization, only Norway had gestational age specified as an eligibility criterion (before 32 weeks’ gestation). Finland, Estonia, Poland, Portugal, and Switzerland offered immunization to all children, while Denmark, France, Germany, Sweden, and the UK targeted high-risk groups without mentioning gestational age. Nilsen concluded that there was “substantial variation in immunization strategies” across the countries studied for preterm children, and that “protection beyond infancy was limited, especially for RSV,” despite these children being at higher risk. “So we believe that there is still room to improve protection against RSV and influenza in children born preterm.” Session co-chair Jasna Petrić Duvnjak, MD, a pediatric pulmonologist at Poliklinika Pediatri in Split, Croatia, asked what Nilsen thought were the “main barriers to achieving equal protection across the European countries,” suggesting that they could be political, financial, or cultural. Nilsen replied that “it’s a complex question with complex answers and you brought some candidates” to the discussion. She pointed out that their mapping showed that the strategies used in each country “are quite different and we don’t really know which one is the best, so maybe that’s also part of the picture.” Expanding on her thoughts, Petrić Duvnjak told Medscape Medical News that she was “surprised” by the range of immunization strategies pursued by the countries included in the study, as “we learn from the same knowledge, and we share our knowledge, we share our experience.” She added: “Why are we so different? Why do we have so different schedules?” Petrić Duvnjak pointed out that immunization guidelines are available, which in theory should guarantee unified practice. She highlighted that “some cultures are reluctant over vaccination or they hesitate, and maybe don’t believe enough in vaccination,” but she said she believes the root cause of the differences is “mostly politics, because the decision-makers are not physicians, they are not clinicians, they are not the ones that are involved in caring for sick children.” The REACH study was funded by Sanofi and AstraZeneca. IMPROVE PRETERM was funded by the European Union’s Horizon Europe Research and Innovation program. Geurtsen is an employee of Sanofi B.V. and holds Sanofi stock options. Other authors of the REACH study are employees of Sanofi and LOGEX. Nilsen reported no relevant financial relationships. Petrić Duvnjak declared no conflicts. 0 Comments Commenting is limited to medical professionals. To comment please . Two European studies showed that national programs using nirsevimab or maternal vaccination against RSV are associated with large reductions in infant hospitalizations. The findings were presented at the CEPAS 2026 Congress. The studies analyzed RSV-related LRTI hospitalizations across four consecutive RSV seasons.

尼塞韦单抗、母亲 疫苗 保护婴儿免受 RSV 相关紧急就诊

Nirsevimab, Maternal Vaccines Protect Infants Against RSV-Related Emergency Visits
新闻简报A study presented at IDWeek 2026 evaluated the effectiveness of nirsevimab and maternal vaccination in reducing RSV-related emergency department (ED) visits among infants during the 2025-2026 respiratory virus season. The research, conducted at six U.S. sites participating in the VISION project, included infants under eight months old with linked maternal records. Using a test-negative, case-control design, the study found that nirsevimab reduced RSV-positive ED encounters by 66% (95% CI, 57-72), while maternal vaccination showed a 63% effectiveness (95% CI, 43-76). The analysis highlighted the importance of monitoring product effectiveness as RSV patterns evolve and new prevention tools emerge. However, the study noted limitations such as possible misclassification of RSV status and residual confounding. These findings suggest that both nirsevimab and maternal vaccination are valuable strategies for protecting infants against RSV, but further research is needed to confirm their long-term impact and address potential biases.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究是在2025年至2026年呼吸道病毒季节期间进行的,结果在2026年10月21日至24日举行的IDWeek 2026上公布。文章发表日期为2026年10月30日。
地点与对象
该研究在参与 VISION 项目的 6 个美国站点进行,重点关注 RSV 季节开始时 8 个月以下的婴儿,这些婴儿在 2025 年 10 月至 2026 年 5 月期间曾因 RSV 样疾病在急诊科 (ED) 就诊,并关联了产妇记录。
事件
研究人员评估了 尼塞韦单抗 和母亲 疫苗接种 在预防婴儿一岁期间与 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 相关的急诊科 (ED) 就诊的实际有效性。该研究采用测试阴性、病例对照设计来比较病例(RSV 抗原或分子检测呈阳性的婴儿)和对照(RSV 阴性婴儿)之间的免疫几率。
规模、影响与风险
在尼塞韦单抗分析中,未接受抗体的婴儿中有24%检测出RSV阳性,而至少在急诊科就诊前7天接受该抗体的婴儿中有12%检测出RSV阳性,产品有效性(PE)为66%(95%置信区间,57-72)。在母体疫苗接种分析中,未接种疫苗的母亲所生婴儿中有31%检测出RSV阳性,而接种疫苗的母亲所生婴儿中有19%检测出RSV阳性,对应的产品有效性(PE)为63%(95%置信区间,43-76)。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究的局限性包括 RSV 状态可能发生错误分类、残留混杂因素以及 clesrovimab 的低摄取率。研究人员强调,由于 RSV 循环模式的变化和新的预防产品,在每个呼吸道病毒季节监测产品有效性的重要性。

Nirsevimab, Maternal Vaccines Protect Infants Against RSV-Related Emergency Visits

News Brief
A study presented at IDWeek 2026 evaluated the effectiveness of nirsevimab and maternal vaccination in reducing RSV-related emergency department (ED) visits among infants during the 2025-2026 respiratory virus season. The research, conducted at six U.S. sites participating in the VISION project, included infants under eight months old with linked maternal records. Using a test-negative, case-control design, the study found that nirsevimab reduced RSV-positive ED encounters by 66% (95% CI, 57-72), while maternal vaccination showed a 63% effectiveness (95% CI, 43-76). The analysis highlighted the importance of monitoring product effectiveness as RSV patterns evolve and new prevention tools emerge. However, the study noted limitations such as possible misclassification of RSV status and residual confounding. These findings suggest that both nirsevimab and maternal vaccination are valuable strategies for protecting infants against RSV, but further research is needed to confirm their long-term impact and address potential biases.

Both infant administration of nirsevimab and maternal immunization effectively reduced the risk for emergency department visits for respiratory syncytial virus during the first year of life. Use of nirsevimab and maternal vaccination against respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) both individually protect infants against RSV-associated emergency department (ED) encounters during their first respiratory virus season, according to study results presented at IDWeek 2026, held from October 21 to 24, in Washington, DC. There are multiple methods that can be used to protect infants against RSV, including long-acting monoclonal antibodies such as nirsevimab and clesrovimab, as well as maternal RSV vaccination. Researchers explored their effectiveness during the 2025 to 2026 respiratory virus season. The researchers sourced data from 6 US sites participating in VISION, which contains electronic health records. The analysis included infants who were younger than 8 months at the start of the RSV season, had an ED encounter for RSV-like illness between October 2025 and May 2026, and had linked maternal records. Monitoring of PE each respiratory virus season is important, as RSV circulation patterns change and new prevention products are introduced. The nirsevimab analysis included 1450 infants who received the antibody at least 7 days prior to ED encounter (with no documented maternal vaccination) and 2309 who did not. The maternal vaccination analysis included 269 infants whose mothers received RSV vaccine at least 14 days prior to birth (with no documented use of monoclonal antibodies) and 352 with unvaccinated mothers. Using a test-negative, case-control design, the researchers calculated product effectiveness (PE) against RSV-associated ED visits by comparing the odds of immunization (ie, either nirsevimab use or maternal vaccination) between cases (those with a positive RSV antigen or molecular test during ED encounters) and control individuals (those who were RSV-negative) in multivariable logistic regression analyses. In the nirsevimab analysis, 24% of infants who did not receive the antibody and 12% of those who received it at least 7 days prior to ED encounter tested positive for RSV, equating to a PE of 66% (95% CI, 57-72). PE was greatest when infants received nirsevimab 7 to 59 days prior to ED encounter (80%; 95% CI, 69-87), with that figure falling to 58% (95% CI, 45-68) and 59% (95% CI, 40-72) when the antibody was administered 60 to 119 and 120 to 179 days prior, respectively. In the maternal RSV vaccination analysis, 31% of infants with unvaccinated mothers and 19% of those with vaccinated mothers tested positive for RSV during ED encounter, corresponding to a PE of 63% (95% CI, 43-76). Study limitations include possible misclassification of RSV status, residual confounding, and low uptake of clesrovimab. According to the researchers, “Monitoring of PE each respiratory virus season is important, as RSV circulation patterns change and new prevention products are introduced.” Disclosure: Multiple study authors declared affiliations with biotech, pharmaceutical, and/or device companies. Please see the original reference for a full list of disclosures. Time
The study was conducted during the 2025 to 2026 respiratory virus season, with the results presented at IDWeek 2026, held from October 21 to 24, 2026. The publication date of the article is October 30, 2026.
Location and population
The study was conducted at 6 US sites participating in the VISION project, focusing on infants younger than 8 months at the start of the RSV season, who had an emergency department (ED) encounter for RSV-like illness between October 2025 and May 2026, and had linked maternal records.
Event
Researchers evaluated the real-world effectiveness of nirsevimab and maternal vaccination for preventing infant respiratory syncytial virus (RSV)-related emergency department (ED) visits during the first year of life. The study used a test-negative, case-control design to compare the odds of immunization between cases (infants with positive RSV antigen or molecular test) and controls (RSV-negative infants).
Scale, impact and risk
In the nirsevimab analysis, 24% of infants who did not receive the antibody tested positive for RSV, compared to 12% of those who received it at least 7 days prior to ED encounter, resulting in a product effectiveness (PE) of 66% (95% CI, 57-72). In the maternal vaccination analysis, 31% of infants with unvaccinated mothers tested positive for RSV, compared to 19% of those with vaccinated mothers, corresponding to a PE of 63% (95% CI, 43-76).
Response, status and uncertainty
The study's limitations include possible misclassification of RSV status, residual confounding, and low uptake of clesrovimab. The researchers emphasized the importance of monitoring product effectiveness each respiratory virus season due to changing RSV circulation patterns and new prevention products.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

基于呼出气颗粒(PExA)非侵入性定量下气道抗原特异性抗体:呼吸道感染的观察性概念验证研究 - 生物通

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 3 日发表在 eBioMedicine 上的一项研究探索了使用呼出气颗粒 (PExA) 无创量化下呼吸道中的抗原特异性抗体。该研究涉及瑞典 28 名医护人员,其中 18 人通过 RT-qPCR 确认感染 RSV、SARS-CoV-2 或甲型/乙型流感。PExA 样本与血清 IgG 水平呈强相关性(Spearman's rho >0.85,p < 0.0001),与 IgA 呈中度相关性。鼻分泌物中的 IgA/IgG 比率显着高于 PExA 样本,与上呼吸道和下呼吸道之间的预期免疫球蛋白梯度一致。这项在斯德哥尔摩 Danderyd 医院进行的研究证明了这种方法的可行性,但强调需要进一步研究以验证其在粘膜 疫苗 开发中的更广泛应用和转化价值。研究结果表明,PExA 可以克服评估下气道免疫力方面长期存在的挑战,支持局部抗体动态的重复采样和纵向分析。
查看新闻五要素
时间
提供的证据没有以经过验证的英语清楚地报告事件时间。
地点与对象
瑞典,斯德哥尔摩,Danderyd 医院;28 名医护人员(18 名确诊感染 RSV、SARS-CoV-2 或甲型/乙型流感;10 名在过去 6 个月内没有已知感染)。
事件
《eBioMedicine》上发表的一项研究证明了使用呼出气颗粒 (PExA) 对下呼吸道中抗原特异性抗体进行无创定量的可行性。该研究涉及 28 名医护人员,其中 18 人通过 RT-qPCR 确认感染 RSV、SARS-CoV-2 或甲型/乙型流感。
规模、影响与风险
该研究测量了 PExA 样本中的 IgA 和 IgG 水平,显示尖峰 IgA 的中位变异系数 (CV) 为 3.7%,H1-IgA 为 7.2%,RSV pre-F IgA 为 5.3%。PExA 样本与血清 IgG 呈强相关性(Spearman's rho >0.85,p < 0.0001),与 IgA 呈中度相关性。鼻腔分泌物中的 IgA/IgG 比率(中位数 4.97)明显高于 PExA 样本(中位数 0.56)。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是一项观察性概念验证,研究结果表明 PExA 作为评估下呼吸道免疫力的非侵入性方法的潜力。然而,该方法在评估下一代粘膜 疫苗 方面的更广泛应用和转化价值需要进一步研究。该研究由欧洲研究委员会、瑞典医学研究委员会以及克努特和爱丽丝·瓦伦堡基金会资助。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
基于呼出气颗粒(PExA)非侵入性定量下气道抗原特异性抗体:呼吸道感染的观察性概念验证研究 - 生物通
News Brief
A study published in eBioMedicine on November 3, 2026, explored the use of exhaled breath particles (PExA) to non-invasively quantify antigen-specific antibodies in the lower airway. The research involved 28 healthcare workers in Sweden, with 18 confirmed to have RSV, SARS-CoV-2, or influenza A/B infections via RT-qPCR. PExA samples showed strong correlation with serum IgG levels (Spearman's rho >0.85, p < 0.0001) and moderate correlation with IgA. The IgA/IgG ratio in nasal secretions was significantly higher than in PExA samples, aligning with the expected immunoglobulin gradient between upper and lower airways. The study, conducted at Danderyd Hospital in Stockholm, demonstrated the feasibility of this method but emphasized the need for further research to validate its broader application and translational value in mucosal vaccine development. The findings suggest that PExA could overcome long-standing challenges in assessing lower airway immunity, supporting repeated sampling and longitudinal analysis of local antibody dynamics.
Original source text (Chinese)
基于呼出气颗粒(PExA)非侵入性定量下气道抗原特异性抗体:呼吸道感染的观察性概念验证研究 《eBioMedicine》:Non-invasive quantification of lower airway antigen-specific antibodies using exhaled breath particles – an observational proof-of-concept study 【字体: 大 中 小 】 时间:2026年11月03日 来源:eBioMedicine 11.2 为了促进科学的传播,生物通采集了这篇文章 订阅BioHot点这里解锁原文链接推送! “畅读会员”点此登录查看原文链接 Open access 背景:黏膜疫苗进展使上、下呼吸道免疫反应评估的实际方法需求日益迫切。虽然鼻采样正逐步标准化,但一直缺少可直接评估远端气道抗原特异性抗体的非侵入性技术。方法:研究人员采用颗粒呼出气(Particles in Exhaled Air,PExA)捕获雾化呼出的呼吸道黏膜衬液(respiratory tract lining fluid,RTLF),以定量抗原特异性免疫球蛋白A(immunoglobulin A,IgA)和免疫球蛋白G(immunoglobulin G,IgG)。在2025年春季,研究人员采集28名医疗保健人员(healthcare workers,HCWs)的配对血清、鼻分泌物和PExA样本,其中18名患有逆转录定量聚合酶链式反应(reverse-transcription quantitative polymerase chain reaction,RT-qPCR)确诊的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2(severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2,SARS-CoV-2)、甲型流感H 1 N 1 或呼吸道合胞病毒(respiratory syncytial virus,RSV)感染。结果:PExA样本中抗原特异性IgA和IgG测量显示较低变异系数(coefficient of variation,CV;中位CV:spike-IgA 3.7%,H 1 -IgA 7.2%,RSV pre-F-IgA 5.3%)。PExA与血清IgG之间呈强相关(Spearman's rho >0.85,p < 0.0001),这与系统性来源可能参与下气道IgG水平一致。相比之下,IgA相关性较温和,这可能反映肺部局部IgA生成。IgG和IgA在PExA与鼻分泌物之间亦相关,而鼻分泌物中的IgA/IgG比值更高(中位5.0对0.6,p < 0.0001),这与上、下呼吸道预期的同种型分布一致。解释:本研究证明可在下气道非侵入性测量抗原特异性抗体。该方法提出了一个评估下气道免疫并支持下一代黏膜疫苗研发的框架。资助:本研究由欧洲研究理事会、瑞典医学研究学会以及Knut与Alice Wallenberg基金会资助。 呼吸道黏膜抗体是抵御气载病原体的基础成分。当前肌肉注射疫苗可预防重症,却难以在病毒入侵部位诱发强抗体,因而对感染和传播的保护有限;COVID-19疫情推动的黏膜疫苗研发使这一缺口更突出。上气道鼻拭子和鼻吸附采样正逐步标准化,下气道取样却长期依赖支气管镜和支气管肺泡灌洗(bronchoalveolar lavage,BAL),属于有创操作;诱导痰主要反映近端大气道且可能受上气道污染;呼出气冷凝(exhaled breath condensate,EBC)受采集条件影响、稀释因子不确定,也不特异来自远端气道。因此,重症感染关键部位的下气道抗原特异性抗体一直缺乏非侵入评估。 义翘神州搭建有三大膜蛋白表达技术平台:VLP平台、去垢剂平台和Nanodisc平台,欢迎下载详细技术资料! 领 取 研究人员利用PExA开展观察性概念验证。该技术在标准化呼吸动作中使小气道闭合后再开放,产生的气溶胶液滴主要来自远端气道RTLF。研究对象来自COMMUNITY医疗工作者队列:2020年4月在瑞典斯德哥尔摩Danderyd医院招募2149名医疗工作者,定期采集血液、唾液和鼻分泌物并进行RT-qPCR监测。2025年春季,研究纳入28名配对样本者,其中18名为RT-qPCR确诊的SARS-CoV-2、流感A/B或RSV感染者,10名为前6个月无已知感染者。研究在感染后1至3个月配对测量血清、鼻分泌物和PExA中抗spike、H 1 及RSV pre-F的IgA/IgG,并结合精密度、相关性和同种型比值判断可行性。论文发表于《eBioMedicine》。 关键方法为PExA标准化呼吸采样:受试者鼻夹固定,经口含咬嘴吸入高效过滤(high efficiency particulate arresting,HEPA)空气,呼气通过惯性撞击器将0.4–7 μm颗粒收集到干燥血斑(dried blood spot,DBS)膜;动作包括呼气至残气量、屏气5 s、最大吸气至肺总量,再呼气至功能残气量,重复至累计240 ng颗粒。收集基质经提取,并按收集颗粒质量归一化。抗体定量采用高灵敏电化学发光免疫分析(electrochemiluminescence immunoassay,ECLIA),并以总IgA/IgG校正。统计分析使用技术重复计算变异系数(coefficient of variation,CV)、Spearman等级相关、Wilcoxon符号秩检验,以及作为稳健性的符号检验。 感染诱导的鼻部分泌型抗体反应:通过比较感染前基线与感染后30天和90天鼻分泌物,发现感染后鼻抗原特异性IgA水平升高;以感染后30天计,SARS-CoV-2、流感A和RSV分别中位升高约9倍、12倍和29倍,抗原特异性IgG仅中度升高。后续感染者的基线鼻抗原特异性IgA低于未感染者,但因样本量小,该观察仅作探索性描述。 PExA样本抗体测量的精密度:对高于检测限(limit of detection,LOD)的技术重复计算CV,spike、H 1 和RSV pre-F特异性IgA的中位CV分别为3.7%、7.2%和5.3%,IgG分别为4.2%、5.2%和3.2%,表明该非侵入样本中的抗原特异性抗体测量具有可重复性。 PExA样本、鼻分泌物与血清的相关性:Spearman等级相关显示,PExA与血清IgG在三种抗原特异性上均强相关(rho >0.85,p < 0.0001);PExA与血清IgA的相关较弱,其中SARS-CoV-2和流感A显著,RSV未达显著。鼻分泌物与血清之间也呈IgG强于IgA的相关模式。PExA与鼻分泌物之间,IgG和IgA均相关,且IgG相关 consistently 更强。 总免疫球蛋白与IgA/IgG比值:总IgA和总IgG在鼻分泌物中均高于PExA样本;鼻分泌物IgA/IgG中位比值为4.97,PExA样本为0.56,配对比较p < 0.0001,符号检验中27对配对差异全部为正。该结果符合上呼吸道以IgA为主、下呼吸道以IgG为主的免疫梯度。 讨论部分强调,上、下气道免疫特征不同,不能将上气道反应直接外推至下气道。肌肉注射主要经血管内渗引起下气道IgG,而黏膜感染或疫苗可在组织驻留浆细胞作用下局部产生分泌型IgA;鼻内接种产生的液滴较大,主要沉积于上气道,气溶胶递送则更易到达下级气道,因此上下气道的保护相关物可能不同。PExA样本的下气道来源虽未在本研究中直接验证,但既往生化与蛋白质组分析显示其与BALF蛋白谱相似、可检出肺表面活性蛋白A(surfactant protein A,SP-A)且未检出唾液淀粉酶;本研究中的IgA/IgG梯度也支持这一解释。局限包括样本量小、女性占多数、仅检测抗体水平而未测中和等功能活性、未包含免疫细胞,且无法区分感染与疫苗诱导的贡献;研究对象也以病毒感染者为主,向细菌性呼吸道感染外推仍需验证。研究结论翻译:该研究提供证据,表明可利用呼出气颗粒在下气道非侵入性定量抗原特异性抗体;该方法有望克服评估下气道免疫的长期障碍,支持重复采样和局部抗体动力学纵向刻画,但仍需进一步研究确立其更广泛应用及在下一代黏膜疫苗评价中的转化价值。 呼吸道黏膜抗体是抵御气载病原体的基础成分。当前肌肉注射疫苗可预防重症,却难以在病毒入侵部位诱发强抗体,因而对感染和传播的保护有限;COVID-19疫情推动的黏膜疫苗研发使这一缺口更突出。上气道鼻拭子和鼻吸附采样正逐步标准化,下气道取样却长期依赖支气管镜和支气管肺泡灌洗(bronchoalveolar View five news elements
Time
The supplied evidence does not clearly report the event time in verified English.
Location and population
Sweden, Stockholm, Danderyd Hospital; 28 healthcare workers (18 with confirmed RSV, SARS-CoV-2, or influenza A/B infections; 10 without known infections in the prior 6 months).
Event
A study published in eBioMedicine demonstrated the feasibility of non-invasive quantification of antigen-specific antibodies in the lower airway using exhaled breath particles (PExA). The research involved 28 healthcare workers, with 18 confirmed to have RSV, SARS-CoV-2, or influenza A/B infections via RT-qPCR.
Scale, impact and risk
The study measured IgA and IgG levels in PExA samples, showing a median coefficient of variation (CV) of 3.7% for spike-IgA, 7.2% for H1-IgA, and 5.3% for RSV pre-F IgA. PExA samples showed strong correlation with serum IgG (Spearman's rho >0.85, p < 0.0001) and moderate correlation with IgA. The IgA/IgG ratio in nasal secretions was significantly higher (median 4.97) compared to PExA samples (median 0.56).
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was an observational proof-of-concept, with findings suggesting the potential of PExA as a non-invasive method for assessing lower airway immunity. However, the method's broader application and translational value in evaluating next-generation mucosal vaccines require further research. The study was funded by the European Research Council, Swedish Medical Research Council, and Knut and Alice Wallenberg Foundation.

东南公共卫生部门指出呼吸道疾病季节即将来临

Southeast Public Health points to impending respiratory illness season
新闻简报东南公共卫生局 (SEPH) 发布了一份媒体新闻稿,强调呼吸道疾病季节的临近,并敦促居民采取预防措施预防季节性病毒性疾病,包括流感、COVID-19 和 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV)。该新闻稿强调了年度流感和 COVID-19 疫苗接种 的重要性,这些疾病对安大略省 6 个月及以上的个人免费。SEPH 卫生副医疗官 Ethan Toumishey 博士表示,这些 疫苗 是保护个人及其亲人免受严重呼吸道疾病并在一年中这个繁忙时期保障医疗保健能力的最佳方式。该建议特别指出,流感和 COVID-19 都可能导致严重的健康后果,特别是对于患有基础疾病的人、65 岁以上的人和幼儿而言。该信息发布于 2026 年 11 月 2 日,最后更新时间为下午 3:22。同一天。所提供的证据中没有报告具体的 暴发 或 RSV 确诊病例,但该建议强调,随着季节的进展,需要保持警惕并采取预防措施。
查看新闻五要素
时间
随着呼吸道疾病季节的临近,该地区的公共卫生部门提醒人们采取尽可能多的措施,防止流感、COVID-19 和 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 等季节性病毒性疾病的感染和传播。
地点与对象
在周一的媒体发布中,东南公共卫生 (SEPH) 指示人们采取预防措施,接种流感和 COVID-19 疫苗(疫苗),该疫苗向安大略省 6 个月及以上的任何人免费提供。
事件
东南公共卫生 (SEPH) 提醒人们采取措施防止季节性病毒性疾病的感染和传播,例如流感、COVID-19 和 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV)。
规模、影响与风险
“流感和 COVID-19 都会造成严重的健康后果,特别是对于有基础疾病的人、65 岁以上的人和幼儿来说。”
应对、状态与不确定性
最后更新时间:2026 年 11 月 2 日下午 3:22
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Southeast Public Health points to impending respiratory illness season

News Brief
Southeast Public Health (SEPH) has issued a media release highlighting the approach of respiratory illness season and urging residents to take preventive measures against seasonal viral illnesses, including influenza, COVID-19, and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV). The release emphasizes the importance of annual flu and COVID-19 vaccinations, which are free for individuals aged six months and older in Ontario. Dr. Ethan Toumishey, SEPH’s deputy medical officer of health, stated that these vaccines are the best way to protect individuals and their loved ones from serious respiratory illness and to safeguard healthcare capacity during this busy time of year. The advisory specifically notes that both the flu and COVID-19 can lead to severe health outcomes, particularly for those with underlying medical conditions, individuals over 65, and young children. The information was published on November 2, 2026, and last updated at 3:22 p.m. on the same day. No specific outbreak or confirmed cases of RSV were reported in the supplied evidence, but the advisory underscores the need for vigilance and preventive actions as the season progresses.

天气转凉,家长注意:呼吸道合胞病毒流行趋势在变,1岁内婴儿尽早预防

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

天气转凉,合胞病毒流行季即将到来。日前举行的中华预防医学会第二届公共卫生监测学术会议上,上海市疾病预防控制中心孙晓冬主任团队以壁报形式发布尼塞韦单抗注射后300天内对婴儿呼吸道合胞病毒(简称RSV)相关下呼吸道感染住院的真实世界保护效果。数据显示,婴儿单次注射尼塞韦单抗后300天,保护效果为87.02%。这是中国首次系统报告尼塞韦单抗在中国婴儿人群中超过180天保护效果的初步数据。

秋冬呼吸道合胞病毒进入活跃期,广州“医院+社区”双通道开放进口长效单抗接种

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

秋冬季节来临,呼吸道传染病进入高发时段,婴幼儿时期感染率最高的病原体之一、呼吸道合胞病毒也迎来活跃期。10月28日,广州市天河区天园街道社区卫生服务中心全科门诊,一对年轻父母带着出生刚满1个月的宝宝,完成了长效呼吸道合胞病毒单抗——克莱罗韦单抗注射液接种。这也是该单抗作为进口创新一类生物制品落地广州后,在社区接种点的首批接种之一。
